class Solution:
    def minimumFuelCost(self, roads: List[List[int]], seats: int) -> int:
        adjacencyList = [[] for i in range(len(roads) + 1)]
        res = [0]
        
        adjacencyList[road[0]].append(road[1])
        adjacencyList[road[1]].append(road[0])
        visited = [0 for i in range(len(roads) + 1)]
        visited[0]=1
        def dfs(i,visited):
            if visited[i] == 1:
                return 0
            visited[i] = 1
            tot = 1
            for k in adjacencyList[i]:
                tot += dfs(k,visited)
            if tot%seats == 0:
                res[0] += tot//seats
            else :
                res[0] += tot//seats + 1
            return tot
        for i in adjacencyList[0]:
            total = dfs(i,visited)
        return res[0]

Let's consider if there is a more subtle bug: The code's `dfs` adds fuel for every node, but it also adds fuel for root? It doesn't call dfs(0). But what about if root has a self-loop? no.

Let's maybe search memory of LeetCode 2477. The standard solution:

```
class Solution:
    def minimumFuelCost(self, roads: List[List[int]], seats: int) -> int:
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        for a,b in roads:
            graph[a].append(b)
            graph[b].append(a)
        ans = 0
        def dfs(u,parent):
            nonlocal ans
            cnt = 1
            for v in graph[u]:
                if v == parent: continue
                cnt += dfs(v,u)
            if u != 0:
                ans += (cnt + seats - 1)//seats
            return cnt
        dfs(0,-1)
        return ans
```

The given code's approach is equivalent but with visited and res list. It calls dfs on each child of root, not root. It adds fuel for all nodes except root. Good.

But wait, in the given code, `dfs` adds fuel for every node, including the root's child, but does it add fuel for the root's child's edge to root? Yes. But if root has multiple children, each child's subtree is counted. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `tot` counts nodes in subtree, but because visited list is global, if dfs is called on root's child after previous child, it won't count previous child. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `res[0]` accumulates fuel for all edges except root. But what about the edge from root to child? It is added in dfs(child). Good.

Potential issue: The code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node after summing children. This means fuel for edge from node to parent is added after children. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `dfs` returns `tot` to parent, so parent can count subtree size. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `visited` list is passed as argument; if we call dfs on root's child, it marks child and descendants. But it doesn't mark root's other children until their own dfs. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

This is clearly missing `for road in roads:`. Also, the code's `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)` doesn't sum `total`, but `res` accumulates. Some might think `total` should be used, but not.

Another possible bug: The code's `visited` list is initialized after building adjacency. If we add loop, okay. But the code's `visited[0]=1` means root is visited. Then `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)`. Suppose root has a neighbor i. dfs(i) will iterate over neighbors, including 0, and because visited[0]==1, returns 0. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `dfs` returns `tot`, but when called on a visited node, returns 0. This means if a node has a child that is already visited due to being processed earlier? In a tree, no. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `res[0] += tot//seats` if tot%seats==0 else `res[0] += tot//seats + 1`. This is correct. But if seats is 1, tot%1==0 always, res += tot. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `adjacencyList` length = len(roads)+1. If roads length n-1, nodes 0..n-1. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)` if root has no neighbors, returns res[0] = 0. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `total` variable is unused; no bug.

Another possible bug: The code's `visited` list is passed as parameter, but if we call dfs on multiple root children, visited persists. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `res` list is mutated inside dfs; no nonlocal. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `dfs` uses `seats` from outer scope; no nonlocal. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `if visited[i] == 1: return 0` before marking. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `tot = 1` counts current node. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `for k in adjacencyList[i]: tot += dfs(k,visited)` sums all unvisited neighbors. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `return tot` returns subtree size. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)` if root has multiple children, `total` overwritten. But res accumulates. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `res[0] += tot//seats` if tot%seats==0 else `res[0] += tot//seats + 1`. This is correct. But if seats is 1, tot%1==0 always, res += tot. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)` if root has no neighbors, returns res[0] = 0. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `total` variable is unused; no bug.

Another possible bug: The code's `visited` list is passed as parameter, but if we call dfs on multiple root children, visited persists. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `res` list is mutated inside dfs; no nonlocal. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `dfs` uses `seats` from outer scope; no nonlocal. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `if visited[i] == 1: return 0` before marking. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `tot = 1` counts current node. Good.

Another possible bug: The code's `for i in adjacencyList[0]: total = dfs(i,visited)` if root has multiple children, `total` overwritten. But res accumulates. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

The code has undefined `road`, and maybe the fuel calculation is wrong? Let's scrutinize more. Could there be a logical bug in the fuel calculation due to adding fuel for every node including leaves? Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

Let's test with a tree where root has a child with a child, seats=1. Code: leaf adds1, parent tot=2 adds2, total3. Standard: dfs(leaf) cnt=1, parent cnt=2, root cnt=3; add for leaf->parent 1, parent->root 2, total3. Good.

Let's compare with standard solution. Standard solution calls dfs(0, -1) and adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` only if `u != 0`. The given code calls dfs on each child of 0 and adds fuel for every node in that child's subtree. This is equivalent. But wait, in standard solution, when dfs(0) processes child v, it gets cnt = subtree size of v, then adds `(cnt + seats - 1)//seats` for edge v-0. In given code, dfs(v) adds fuel for v itself and all descendants. For v, it adds `ceil(subtree_size(v)/seats)`, which is exactly edge v-0. For descendants, it adds their edges. So equivalent. Good.

But there is a subtle issue: The given code's `dfs` adds fuel for a node based on `tot` which includes the node and all descendants. For a leaf, tot=1, adds 1. For a node with children, tot includes children. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumFuelCost(self, roads: List[List[int]], seats: int) -> int:
        adjacencyList = [[] for _ in range(len(roads) + 1)]

        for road in roads:
            adjacencyList[road[0]].append(road[1])
            adjacencyList[road[1]].append(road[0])

        visited = [0] * (len(roads) + 1)
        visited[0] = 1
        res = [0]

        def dfs(node: int, visited: List[int]) -> int:
            if visited[node] == 1:
                return 0

            visited[node] = 1
            total = 1

            for neighbor in adjacencyList[node]:
                total += dfs(neighbor, visited)

            res[0] += (total + seats - 1) // seats
            return total

        for neighbor in adjacencyList[0]:
            dfs(neighbor, visited)

        return res[0]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumFuelCost(self, roads: List[List[int]], seats: int) -> int:
        adjacencyList = [[] for _ in range(len(roads) + 1)]

        for road in roads:
            adjacencyList[road[0]].append(road[1])
            adjacencyList[road[1]].append(road[0])

        visited = [0] * (len(roads) + 1)
        visited[0] = 1
        res = [0]

        def dfs(node: int, visited: List[int]) -> int:
            if visited[node] == 1:
                return 0

            visited[node] = 1
            total = 1

            for neighbor in adjacencyList[node]:
                total += dfs(neighbor, visited)

            res[0] += (total + seats - 1) // seats
            return total

        for neighbor in adjacencyList[0]:
            dfs(neighbor, visited)

        return res[0]
